New York Jets Performance Lab 2026

This overview brings the Jets’ 2026 season together in one place. The charts show how the offence and defence have performed week by week, using EPA per play and success rate, with a closer look at passing and rushing. The game-by-game table adds the opponent, number of plays and key efficiency figures, so you can see where performances have improved, dipped or varied from one matchup to the next. The analysis covers standard run and pass plays; defensive EPA is points allowed per play, so lower numbers are better. Play-by-play data can show what happened on the field, but it cannot reliably identify coverage calls, route assignments, blocking schemes or individual player grades.

Weekly readout

  • Through Week 9, offence is +0.003 EPA/play (rank 14/32); defence allows +0.039 EPA/play (rank 22/32, lower is better).
  • Last-three-game EPA/play: offence +0.062; defensive EPA allowed +0.220.
  • Largest opponent-adjusted defensive passing outlier: Week 7 vs MIA, +0.661 EPA/play above that opponent baseline. Descriptive only, not a full schedule-strength model.
  • Average defensive QB-hit rate over latest three charted weeks: 18.4%; not a player-level pass-rush grade.
  • Next review: check whether defensive EPA allowed stabilises, monitor second-down and explosive-pass performance, and track snap-share/injury changes.
  • 2026 play-level coverage shells, exact personnel packages and defender-to-receiver assignments are unavailable in the current free feed.

Defensive Coverage Intelligence

Directly charted pass-dropback labels joined to play-by-play. Historical coverage is available through 2025; 2026 play-level coverage calls are not released until after the postseason. 2016–2022 labels originate from NFL Next Gen Stats via nflverse; 2023 onward labels originate from FTN Data via nflverse. Treat the provider transition as a methodological break. Rates use labelled plays as denominators; EPA and pressure are descriptive, not causal. No coverage labels are inferred.

Advanced data availability and limitations

The following fields are not available in the current free 2026 play-by-play source. No proxy assignment or invented personnel label is used.

offense_personnelNot present in the current 2026 PBP file
defense_personnelNot present in the current 2026 PBP file
offense_player_participationNo complete on-field player list per play in the current PBP file
defense_player_participationNo complete on-field player list per play in the current PBP file
coverage_assignmentNo play-level defender-to-receiver responsibility field
coverage_shell_2026No play-level coverage label in the current free 2026 feed
  • Weather splits are game-level and descriptive, not causal
  • No player-combination impact is calculated without complete per-play participation
  • No personnel-package split is calculated without explicit package fields
  • No coverage responsibility is inferred from tackles, interceptions or proximity

Expanding-window historical backtest

Lower MAE and RMSE indicate better forecasts. Every prediction uses only games completed before its target week.

Validation: The backtest does not show a consistent advantage over the league-average baseline. Treat these forecasts as exploratory context, not a validated predictive model; the model should not drive decisions on its own.

side model observations mae rmse mean_error
defense blended_recent_and_season 176 0.161 0.207 0.017
defense team_season_average 176 0.166 0.210 0.019
defense league_average 176 0.162 0.203 0.021
offense blended_recent_and_season 176 0.159 0.202 0.018
offense team_season_average 176 0.161 0.206 0.019
offense league_average 176 0.162 0.203 0.021

Model: 60% play-weighted mean of latest three team games + 40% play-weighted season-to-date mean; shrink 20% toward play-weighted league mean.

  • Early-season sample sizes are small and forecasts are descriptive baselines, not calibrated probabilities
  • Backtest uses expanding time windows and excludes the target week from all inputs
  • EPA is volatile; the model should only be retained if it improves on the league-average baseline
  • Defensive EPA is opponent EPA allowed per play; lower is better

Interpretation limits

  • Pressure deployment splits are descriptive associations, not causal estimates
  • n_blitzers counts blitzers; n_pass_rushers counts all pass rushers. A five-rusher play commonly includes four defensive linemen plus one blitzer.
  • Small categories should not be interpreted without considering their play counts
  • Snap counts measure player deployment and are not per-play player attribution or coverage responsibility
  • A QB hit or sack event is not a complete pressure chart or pass-rush win measure

Coverage attribution and limits

  • Only 2026 FTN-charted passing plays matched to nflverse PBP are used for deployment splits.
  • Opponent adjustment is descriptive and does not control for quarterback, injuries, game state, protection or play-calling.
  • Pressure counts describe rush deployment, not time-to-pressure or individual pass-rush wins.
  • Explosive pass is defined as 20+ yards; YAC explosive proxy is 10+ yards after catch.
  • No reliable public 2026 play-level coverage-shell or defender-to-receiver assignment field was found in the available sources; coverage effectiveness cannot be attributed to individual defenders or shells.
  • Small deployment and situational groups should be interpreted cautiously.

Interpretation limits

  • PFR snap counts show player exposure by game, not the exact 11 defenders on each charted passing play.
  • Weekly position-group snap shares are aggregate exposure and cannot establish which personnel were on the field for a given play.
  • Injury report statuses are not treated as proof of game absence; actual snap counts are the better participation measure.
  • Week-level relationships are descriptive and are not causal player-impact estimates.
  • Injury-to-snap reconciliation uses normalized names; unmatched injury entries are retained.
  • Zero recorded defensive snaps does not prove a player was inactive; the snap feed cannot distinguish inactivity from non-use.
  • The current public FTN 2026 feed has no reliable play-level defender participation or coverage responsibility assignments.

Data coverage and limitations

Matched player-weeks: 140 of 159 (88.1%).

  • Weekly defensive statistics are official box-score production, not charted player-level pressure or coverage impact.
  • QB hits and sacks are recorded outcomes; they do not capture every pressure or pass-rush win.
  • Rates per 100 defensive snaps are descriptive workload normalizations, not causal or position-adjusted player grades.
  • Tackles and passes defended depend on role, opponent play volume and opportunity; comparisons across positions are not direct quality rankings.
  • Weekly GSIS player IDs are used for the primary snap-to-stat join; records without a usable ID remain unmatched and are counted in status.

Interpretation limits

  • PFR snap counts measure player exposure by game, not exact personnel group or formation on each play.
  • Position-group shares are sums of player snaps; because multiple players share a play, interpret as workload composition rather than mutually exclusive shares of 11 players.
  • Injury statuses are not proof of game absence; snap counts are used as observed participation.
  • Zero recorded offensive snaps does not prove a player was inactive.
  • Injury-to-snap reconciliation uses normalized player names; unmatched entries are retained.
  • Offensive line player IDs may be missing in the PFR-to-GSIS crosswalk; unmatched production is reported separately.

Data coverage and limitations

Matched player-weeks: 100 of 155 (64.5%).

  • Official weekly box-score and EPA fields are combined with PFR offensive snap exposure.
  • Snap-normalized rates describe output per workload; they are not player grades or causal estimates.
  • Quarterback passing EPA per attempt is a separate efficiency measure; yards per snap is not a quarterback efficiency metric.
  • Receiving yards per target and rushing/receiving production depend on role, play design and opportunity.
  • Offensive line output is assessed through snap exposure and team-level results; individual blocking grades are not available in the free sources.
  • Stable GSIS IDs are used where available; normalized exact names are used only for snap records without a usable ID. Unmatched records are counted.
PAGES

Analysis controls

Filters apply to visible dashboard tables where matching columns exist.

Player evaluation — role-adjusted measures

Offence: compare QB passing EPA/attempt; RB yards and touches per 100 snaps; WR/TE receiving yards per target and targets per 100 snaps. Defence: compare DL disruption events and QB hits per 100 snaps; LB/DB tackles, passes defended and interceptions alongside snap share. Do not rank OL on box-score rates: individual blocking grades are unavailable.

Rates are workload normalisations, not grades or causal estimates. Consider games played, role, opportunity and matched-week coverage.

Data confidence and limitations

AreaConfidenceInterpretation
Play-level EPA and weekly performanceHighPBP play-level team/game outcomes
Player production and snap exposureLimitedDescriptive rates; incomplete OL matching
Defensive pressure chartingLimitedFTN charted subset, not complete pressure attribution
2026 coverage schemeUnavailablePublished team aggregate only; no play-level labels
Personnel packages and assignmentsUnavailableNo complete per-play participation or responsibility

Confidence refers to field availability and granularity, not performance quality.

Pro Football Reference advanced player statistics

Free PFR advanced-stat feeds distributed through nflverse. These are player-level supplementary measures; coverage and pressure fields are shown only where the source supplies them. Missing source fields are not estimated.

ESPN Total QBR — Jets quarterbacks

ESPN Total QBR is a separate, context-adjusted quarterback metric. It is not the traditional passer rating and should be read alongside EPA/play, completion efficiency, pressure and the team’s overall offensive output.
